<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We give a negative answer to Hilbert's tenth problem over the rational numbers: no algorithm decides whether a polynomial with integer coefficients has a rational zero. The number of variables is part of the input.

- **claim-rational-undecidability** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is no algorithm which, given a polynomial $f\in\mathbb Z[X_1,\ldots,X_n]$, with $n$ part of the input, decides whether $f$ has a zero in $\mathbb Q^n$.
  - Explanation: Rational polynomial solvability is undecidable when the number of variables is part of the input.
- **claim-fixed-elliptic-rank** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ell-rank)): The group $E(F)$ has rank one. Consequently there exist a positive integer $m$ and a nontorsion point $P\in E(F)$ such that $$mE(F)=\mathbb ZP.$$ We fix such $m$ and $P$ once and for all.
  - Explanation: The fixed elliptic group supplies a cyclic subgroup generated by a nontorsion point, giving unique integer indices for the point assignments.
- **claim-formal-index-ratios** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-ell-formal)): There is a fixed finite set of rational primes $S_E$ with the following properties. Let $p\notin S_E$, let $v$ be any place of $F$ above $p$, normalized by $v(p)=1$, and let $n,n'\in\mathbb Z$, with $n'\ne0$. Assume $nP,n'P$ reduce to $O$ at $v$. Then $$\label{ell:ratio} v\!\left(\frac{z(nP)}{z(n'P)}\right)=v_p(n/n').$$ For every integer $K\ge1$, if in addition $p>K+1$ and $v_p(n/n')\ge0$, then $\ell_K(z(n'P))\ne0$ and $$\label{ell:truncation} v\!\left(\frac{\ell_K(z(nP))}{\ell_K(z(n'P))}-\frac n{n'}\right) \ge K.$$ Both assertions also hold at the same place $v$ after replacing $E,P,\ell_K$ by $E^\sigma,P^\sigma,\ell_K^\sigma$ and imposing the corresponding reduction-to-$O$ hypotheses on the conjugate points. Here and below the valuation of zero is $+\infty$, so $n=0$ is included.
  - Explanation: Formal parameters recover valuations of index ratios. Once the ratio is integral, a finite logarithm truncation approximates it to the prescribed precision. The conjugate curve satisfies the same assertions.
- **claim-paired-local-comparison** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-red-local-comparison)): Let $K\ge1$ be an ordinary integer. Let $q$ be a prime of the ambient integer sort ${}^*\mathbb Z$, possibly nonordinary, with $q\notin S_E$ and $q>K+1$, and let $v_*$ be a place of ${}^*F$ above $q$, normalized by $v_*(q)=1$. Suppose there are nonzero $h,j_0\in R$, chosen independently of $a$, such that for every $a\in R$:* 1. *The points $\widetilde T_h,\widetilde T_{ha}$ reduce to $O$ at $v_*$, the value $\ell_K(z(\widetilde T_h))$ is nonzero, and $$v_*\!\left( \frac{\ell_K(z(\widetilde T_{ha}))}{\ell_K(z(\widetilde T_h))} -\widetilde a\right)\ge K.$$* 2. *The points $\widetilde T_{j_0}^{\sigma}$ and $\widetilde T_{(h-4)a}^{\sigma}$ reduce to $O$ on the conjugate curve at the same place $v_*$, and $$v_*\!\left( \frac{z(\widetilde T_{(h-4)a}^{\sigma})} {z(\widetilde T_{j_0}^{\sigma})}\right)\ge K.$$* *Then every $\widetilde a$ is integral at $q$, and $$v_q(\widetilde a-\eta(a))\ge K \qquad(a\in R).$$ Here $z(O)=0$. The denominator in (ii) is nonzero because $j_0\ne0$ and the nonzero points in <a href="#red:elliptic-axioms" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="red:elliptic-axioms">[red:elliptic-axioms]</a> have nonzero $x$- and $y$-coordinates.
  - Explanation: The conjugate comparison first makes the primary index ratio integral and identifies it with the additive index. The primary truncated logarithm then compares that index with the embedded ring value.
- **claim-five-point-height** (bound, [source](#stmt-hei-height)): There exist five distinct points $\mathcal C\subset\mathbf P^1(\mathbb Q)$, including $\infty$, a finite set $S$ of rational primes, and constants $H,c>0$ with the following property. For each $b\in\mathcal C$, choose a primitive integral linear form $L_b(U,V)$ vanishing at $b$, with $L_\infty(U,V)=V$. For $s=(u:v)\in\mathbf P^1(\mathbb Q)\setminus\mathcal C$, let $M_S(s)$ be the product, taken without repetition, of primes $q\notin S$ for which at least one of the integers $$\label{hei:radical} v_q(L_b(u,v))-\min\{v_q(u),v_q(v)\},\qquad b\in\mathcal C,$$ is odd. Here $v_q(q)=1$ and $v_q(0)=+\infty$. Then $$\label{hei:height-bound} h(s)\le H M_S(s)^c.$$
  - Explanation: A fixed power of the product of nonexceptional odd-contact primes bounds the absolute logarithmic height. Contact orders are normalized to remove dependence on homogeneous coordinates.
- **claim-enlarged-height-exceptions** (bound, [source](#stmt-hei-enlarge-exceptions)): If $S'\supset S$ is any fixed finite set of primes, then $$h(s)\le H\left(\prod_{q\in S'\setminus S}q\right)^c M_{S'}(s)^c \qquad(s\in\mathbf P^1(\mathbb Q)\setminus\mathcal C).$$ In particular enlarging the exceptional set changes the constant but leaves the exponent unchanged.
  - Explanation: Removing further fixed primes from the contact radical alters the height constant but preserves the exponent.
- **claim-ordinary-contact-representations** (construction, [source](#stmt-pri-integer-coverage)): Let $\mathcal K\subset\mathbb Z_{>0}$ be the finite multiplier set supplied by Lemma <a href="#pri:pattern" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pri:pattern">29</a> in Section <a href="#pri:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pri:section">6</a>. It has the following property. For every integer $K\ge1$, every $A\in\mathbb Z$, and every real lower bound $Y_0>0$, there exist integers $u_1,u_2,v$, with $v\ne0$, such that, on setting $u_3=Av-u_1-u_2$, the following hold.* 1. *<span class="upright">Representation and primes.</span> Each of the thirteen numbers $$\label{pri:coverage-list} v,\qquad L_b(u_i,v) \quad(1\le i\le3,\ b\in\mathcal C\setminus\{\infty\})$$ equals $\varepsilon k r$, where $\varepsilon\in\{1,-1\}$, $k\in\mathcal K$, and $r>Y_0$ is a positive rational prime. Every such $r$ splits in $F$, is a prime of good reduction for both $E$ and $E^\sigma$, lies outside $S_E$, and satisfies $r>K+1$.* 2. *<span class="upright">Witnesses at each place.</span> For each occurrence of a prime $r$ in this list and each of the two places $v_r$ of $F$ above it, there are nonzero integers $h,j_0$, chosen independently of $a\in\mathbb Z$, such that every $a\in\mathbb Z$ satisfies conditions (i) and (ii) of Lemma <a href="#red:local-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="red:local-comparison">4</a> in the ordinary setting: take $R=\mathbb Z$, its identity embedding in the ordinary structure, $T_a=aP$, $q=r$, and $v_*=v_r$, normalized by $v_r(r)=1$. In this specialization the tildes disappear. The choices of $h,j_0$ may differ at different occurrences and at the two places.* *The set $\mathcal K$ is independent of $K,A,Y_0$.
  - Explanation: Every ordinary integer has a representation by slopes whose contact values use fixed multipliers and sufficiently large split primes. Auxiliary witnesses at each place serve all integer inputs.
- **claim-recursive-standard-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-red-standard-model)): The theory $\mathcal T$ is recursive and has a model whose ring is $\mathbb Z$. In this model $T_a=aP$ for every integer $a$.
  - Explanation: The ring and elliptic axioms, including the representation schemes, admit the ordinary integer model. The theory is recursive after its fixed arithmetic data are chosen.
- **claim-existential-pole-parity** (construction, [source](#stmt-par-pole-parity)): There are a positive existential formula $\Phi(b)$ in the language of rings, with fixed rational coefficients, and a finite set $S_1$ of rational primes such that:* 1. *$\mathbb Q\models\Phi(b)$ for every $b\in\mathbb Z$;* 2. *if $b\in\mathbb Q^\times$ and $\mathbb Q\models\Phi(b)$, then, for every $q\notin S_1$, the inequality $v_q(b)<0$ implies $v_q(b)\in2\mathbb Z$.
  - Explanation: A fixed positive existential condition holds on all integers and prohibits odd negative valuations outside a fixed exceptional set. It does not prohibit even poles.
- **claim-compactness-model** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-red-compactness)): If every finite rational test succeeds, there exist an ambient elementary extension of the many-sorted structure specified in §<a href="#red:local-comparison-section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="red:local-comparison-section">2.1</a>, a model $R$ of $\mathcal T$, a tuple $\mathbf a\in R^n$ with $f(\mathbf a)=0$, and an embedding $R\hookrightarrow{}^*\mathbb Q$ into its rational sort such that every element of the image satisfies $\Phi$.
  - Explanation: Success of every finite test yields an embedded term-generated ring containing a root and satisfying the existential pole condition throughout its image. The elementary diagram is used for existence rather than as an effective input.
- **claim-tested-branch-coarsening** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-red-coarsening)): For the prime $q$ and element $r$ in <a href="#red:odd-r" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="red:odd-r">[red:odd-r]</a>, there is a branch $\mathfrak p\in\{\mathfrak p_+,\mathfrak p_-\}$ of that representation axiom and an ambient place $v_*$ of ${}^*F$ over $q$ such that, for every ordinary integer $l\ge1$ and every $D'\in F_R$, $$\label{red:valuation-transfer} D'\in r^l(R_F)_{\mathfrak p} \quad\Longrightarrow\quad v_*(\widetilde D')\ge l.$$ Every point formal for this tested localization actually reduces to $O$ for $v_*$.
  - Explanation: Pole parity ensures that the odd value of a contact factor survives valuation coarsening. The center selects a tested residue branch and transfers its localization memberships to actual valuation inequalities.
- **claim-contact-index-congruences** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-red-index-congruence)): Fix an ordinary $K\ge1$ and use the exceptional set $S_K$ above. Choose a representation supplied at precision $K$ for an arbitrary $A\in R$, and put $\widetilde s_i=(\widetilde u_i:\widetilde v)$. For every prime $q$ of ${}^*\mathbb Z$ counted by any $M_K(\widetilde s_i)$, and every $a\in R$, the element $\widetilde a$ is integral at $q$ and $$\label{red:index-congruence-equation} v_q(\widetilde a-\eta(a))\ge K.$$
  - Explanation: At every contact prime of any tested representation, all embedded ring values are integral and agree with their additive indices to the chosen precision.
- **claim-finite-test-integer-root** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-red-finite-test-success)): Let $f\in\mathbb Z[X_1,\ldots,X_n]$ be nonconstant. If every finite system of rational constraints constructed in §<a href="#red:finite-tests" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="red:finite-tests">2.4</a> for $f$ is solvable, then $f$ has a zero in $\mathbb Z^n$.
  - Explanation: Solvability of all finite rational constraints forces an ordinary integer root. The proof compares a uniform height bound on every modeled ring value with quadratic elliptic height growth.
- **claim-pole-formula-soundness** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-par-soundness)): For any finite lists as above, if $b\in\mathbb Q^\times$ satisfies $\Phi(b)$ and $q\notin S_1$, then $v_q(b)<0$ implies $v_q(b)\in2\mathbb Z$.
  - Explanation: For arbitrary fixed multiplier lists, an odd pole would make the required coordinate square class incompatible with one of the auxiliary elliptic curves.
- **claim-mutual-square-supports** (theorem, [source](#stmt-par-cross-squares)): The sets $B,J$ are disjoint and avoid $2,3$. Moreover, $$\label{par:mutual-squares} \theta\in\mathbb Q_q^{\times2}\quad(q\in B),\qquad \beta\in\mathbb Q_q^{\times2}\quad(q\in J).$$ Both $\beta$ and $\theta$ are squares in $\mathbb Q_2^\times$; at $3$, $\theta$ is a square and $\beta$ is a unit nonsquare. In particular $B\ne\varnothing$.
  - Explanation: The squarefree representatives have disjoint supports and mutual local square conditions. Their local classes at the exceptional places provide the residue constraints used in descent.
- **claim-selmer-matrix-criterion** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-par-descent)): Let $l>0$ be squarefree, prime to $6$, with $l\equiv3\pmod4$ and $l\equiv2\pmod3$. Suppose that its support $Q$ contains the nonempty set $B$ of Lemma <a href="#par:cross-squares" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="par:cross-squares">15</a> and a prime outside $B$, and suppose that $$\label{par:matrix-target} \ker K=\langle\mathbf1,\mathbf1_B\rangle, \qquad \lambda\notin\operatorname{im}K.$$ Then $$\dim_{\mathbb F_2}\mathop{\mathrm{Sel}}_2(E_l/\mathbb Q)=3,$$ and $(\beta,1)$ is a Selmer class outside the subgroup of rational two-torsion classes, in Kummer coordinates $(x+3l,x-l)$.
  - Explanation: The residue-matrix hypotheses bound the Selmer group and isolate the desired square-class direction outside rational torsion. The proof separately verifies actual local membership of that direction.
- **claim-global-square-class-point** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-par-global-point)): Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#par:descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="par:descent">16</a>, the curve $E_l$ has a rational point with nonzero $x$-coordinate in $\beta\mathbb Q^{\times2}$.
  - Explanation: The Selmer calculation supplies a bound on full Selmer corank. The cited converse gives finiteness of the Tate–Shafarevich group, and the alternating pairing then makes the desired Selmer class come from a rational point.
- **claim-simultaneous-twist-supports** (construction, [source](#stmt-par-simultaneous-matrices)): For $\beta,\theta,B,J$ as in Lemma <a href="#par:cross-squares" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="par:cross-squares">15</a>, there is a positive squarefree integer $n$, prime to $6\theta$, whose support $P$ contains $B$ and at least one auxiliary prime outside $B$, such that the following statements hold for both $l=n$ and $l=n\theta$. The number $l$ satisfies $l\equiv3\pmod4$ and $l\equiv2\pmod3$. On its prime support $Q=P$ or $Q=P\cup J$, the matrices <a href="#par:matrices" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="par:matrices">[par:matrices]</a> satisfy <a href="#par:matrix-target" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="par:matrix-target">[par:matrix-target]</a>.
  - Explanation: Auxiliary primes realize residue symbols making the descent criterion hold for both twists. A nonsingularity argument handles the added support without disturbing the smaller support.
- **claim-odd-contact-fiber-field** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-hei-fiber-field)): For $s\in\mathbf P^1(\mathbb Q)\setminus\mathcal C$, put $M=M_S(s)$. There is a Galois extension $K_0/\mathbb Q$ of uniformly bounded degree splitting the fiber $\pi^{-1}(s)$, unramified outside $S\cup\{q:q\mid M\}$. Every fiber $A=\mathcal A_y$ above $s$ is defined over $K_0$ and has good reduction at every place of $K_0$ outside $S$.
  - Explanation: A rational fiber splits over a field of bounded degree. Outside fixed exceptions, even contact gives unramified local lifts, while the pulled-back abelian scheme has good reduction even where the splitting field ramifies.
- **claim-base-faltings-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-hei-base-height)): For every $y\in Y(\overline\mathbb Q)$ one has $$\label{hei:comparison} h(\pi(y))\le C\bigl(1+\max\{0,h_{\mathrm F}(\mathcal A_y)\}\bigr),$$ with a constant independent of $y$ and its field of definition.
  - Explanation: A nonconstant theta-null map supplies an ample height on a fixed covering curve. Comparison with Faltings height controls the base height independently of the point's field of definition.
- **claim-common-homomorphism-field** (construction, [source](#stmt-hei-hom-field)): There is a Galois extension $L/\mathbb Q$ containing $K_0$, of uniformly bounded degree, over which all geometric homomorphisms between all Galois conjugates of $A$ are defined. It is unramified outside $T$.
  - Explanation: The finite Galois action on the bounded-rank group of geometric homomorphisms produces a common bounded-degree field. Good reduction prevents additional ramification outside the prescribed set.
- **claim-controlled-sign-splitting** (construction, [source](#stmt-hei-sign-splitting)): There is a finite Dirichlet character $\chi$ of order $m\le C M$, unramified outside $T$, and a continuous cochain $\beta:G_\mathbb Q\to\mu_{2m}$ such that $$\label{hei:beta} \beta^2=\chi,\qquad \frac{\beta(g)\beta(h)}{\beta(gh)}=\epsilon(g,h).$$ The cochain can be chosen trivial on inertia at every prime outside $T$. Its restriction to $G_L$ is a conjugacy-invariant character. The local conductor of $\chi$ at any fixed prime is bounded independently of $s$, and $\chi$ is tame at odd primes.
  - Explanation: Local character square-root obstructions match the isogeny sign obstruction. A corrected cochain controls both the extension degree and inertia without requiring bounds on the individual isogeny degrees.
- **claim-coefficient-prime-compatibility** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hei-compatibility)): The representation $r_3$ is the $3$-adic representation of the same algebraic conjugate of $f$ that realizes $r_2$.
  - Explanation: Reduction of the same quasi-isogeny and relative Frobenius gives a common algebraic trace. This identifies the representations at both coefficient primes with the same algebraic conjugate of the newform.
- **claim-modular-level-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-hei-level-bound)): There are fixed constants $C,a>0$ such that $$\label{hei:level} N\le C M^a.$$
  - Explanation: Tame ramification bounds the conductor at varying large primes. Uniform local ramification cuts handle fixed smaller primes, yielding a polynomial level bound.
- **claim-modular-jacobian-factor** (construction, [source](#stmt-hei-modular-factor)): There is an abelian variety $D_0/L'$ such that $$J\sim_{L'}A\times D_0.$$
  - Explanation: A shared representation constituent descends to a nonzero homomorphism over the controlled field. Absolute simplicity and complete reducibility make the surface an isogeny factor of the modular Jacobian.
- **claim-prime-contact-patterns** (construction, [source](#stmt-pri-pattern)): There is a finite set $\mathcal K\subset\mathbb Z_{>0}$ such that, for every $A\in\mathbb Z$ and every real $B>0$, there exist integers $u_1,u_2,v$, with $v\ne0$, for which, on setting $u_3=Av-u_1-u_2$, each of the thirteen numbers $$\label{pri:forms-list} v,\qquad L_b(u_j,v) \quad(1\le j\le3,\ b\in\mathcal C\setminus\{\infty\})$$ has the form $\epsilon kr$, where $\epsilon\in\{1,-1\}$, $k\in\mathcal K$, and $r>B$ is a positive rational prime. Every such prime splits in $F$ and is a prime of good reduction for both $E$ and $E^\sigma$. At either place of $F$ above $r$, if $d_1,d_2$ are the cardinalities of the reduction groups of $E,E^\sigma$ over $\mathbb F_r$, then $$\label{pri:order-conditions} \gcd(d_1,d_2)\mid4, \qquad r\nmid d_1d_2.$$ The set $\mathcal K$ is independent of $A$ and $B$.
  - Explanation: A fixed finite multiplier set permits simultaneous prime contact values for every integer and every lower threshold. Lower prime-pattern counts and upper sieves remove primes with unwanted common reduction-order factors.
- **claim-simultaneous-integer-witnesses** (construction, [source](#stmt-pri-integer-tests)): Fix an integer $K\ge1$ and a prime $r$ supplied by Lemma <a href="#pri:pattern" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="pri:pattern">29</a>, large enough for Proposition <a href="#ell:formal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ell:formal">3</a> at precision $K$. At each of the two places $v$ above $r$ there are nonzero integers $h,j_0$, chosen independently of $a\in\mathbb Z$, such that, for every $a\in\mathbb Z$:* 1. *$hP$ and $haP$ reduce to $O$ at $v$, $\ell_K(z(hP))\ne0$, and $$v\!\left(\frac{\ell_K(z(haP))}{\ell_K(z(hP))}-a\right) \ge K;$$* 2. *$j_0P^\sigma$ and $(h-4)aP^\sigma$ reduce to $O$ at the same place $v$, and $$v\!\left(\frac{z((h-4)aP^\sigma)}{z(j_0P^\sigma)}\right) \ge K.$$* *The choices of $h,j_0$ may differ at the two places.
  - Explanation: Compatible congruences on reduction orders choose auxiliary integers whose local comparisons hold for every integer input at each split place.
- **claim-uniform-upper-sieve** (bound, [source](#stmt-pri-upper-sieve)): The number of points in this square avoiding every $\Omega_p$ is at most $$\label{pri:sieve-bound} \frac{n^2}{G(Z)}+O(nZ^6+Z^8),\qquad G(Z)=\sum_{\substack{e\le Z\text{ squarefree}\\p\mid e\Rightarrow p\in\mathcal P_Z}} \prod_{p\mid e}\frac{g(p)}{1-g(p)}.$$ The error constant is absolute.
  - Explanation: Selberg weights diagonalize the main term and bound residue-count errors uniformly in the position of the square.
- **claim-turing-degree-quartic-normal-form** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-degree-normal-form)): With the usual effective coding, let $\mathrm{H10}(\mathbb Q)$ be the set of integral polynomials having a rational zero, with the number of variables part of the input. Then $\mathrm{H10}(\mathbb Q)$ has Turing degree $0'$, the degree of the halting problem. The same is true for each of the following restricted input problems:* 1. *rational solvability for integral polynomials of total degree at most four;* 2. *given a nonempty finite list $q_1,\ldots,q_s\in\mathbb Z[X_1,\ldots,X_N]$, each of total degree at most two, whether $\sum_{i=1}^s q_i^2$ has a zero in $\mathbb Q^N$.* *No bound is imposed on the number of variables, the number of summands in (ii), or the coefficient heights.
  - Explanation: The finite-test oracle reduction and rational-tuple enumeration identify the Turing degree. Arithmetic-circuit normalization preserves rational solvability while restricting polynomial degree, with unbounded variables and coefficient heights.

Suppose there are nonzero $h,j_0\\in R$, chosen independently of $a$, such that for every $a\\in R$:*\n\n1.  *The points $\\widetilde T_h,\\widetilde T_{ha}$ reduce to $O$ at $v_*$, the value $\\ell_K(z(\\widetilde T_h))$ is nonzero, and $$v_*\\!\\left(\n     \\frac{\\ell_K(z(\\widetilde T_{ha}))}{\\ell_K(z(\\widetilde T_h))}\n                       -\\widetilde a\\right)\\ge K.$$*\n\n2.  *The points $\\widetilde T_{j_0}^{\\sigma}$ and $\\widetilde T_{(h-4)a}^{\\sigma}$ reduce to $O$ on the conjugate curve at the same place $v_*$, and $$v_*\\!\\left(\n     \\frac{z(\\widetilde T_{(h-4)a}^{\\sigma})}\n          {z(\\widetilde T_{j_0}^{\\sigma})}\\right)\\ge K.$$*\n\n*Then every $\\widetilde a$ is integral at $q$, and $$v_q(\\widetilde a-\\eta(a))\\ge K\n                       \\qquad(a\\in R).$$ Here $z(O)=0$. The denominator in (ii) is nonzero because $j_0\\ne0$ and the nonzero points in <a href=\"#red:elliptic-axioms\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"red:elliptic-axioms\">[red:elliptic-axioms]</a> have nonzero $x$- and $y$-coordinates.",
      "explanation": "The conjugate comparison first makes the primary index ratio integral and identifies it with the additive index. The primary truncated logarithm then compares that index with the embedded ring value.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-hei-height",
        "exact_quote": "There exist five distinct points $\\mathcal C\\subset\\mathbf P^1(\\mathbb Q)$, including $\\infty$, a finite set $S$ of rational primes, and constants $H,c>0$ with the following property. For each $b\\in\\mathcal C$, choose a primitive integral linear form $L_b(U,V)$ vanishing at $b$, with $L_\\infty(U,V)=V$. For $s=(u:v)\\in\\mathbf P^1(\\mathbb Q)\\setminus\\mathcal C$, let $M_S(s)$ be the product, taken without repetition, of primes $q\\notin S$ for which at least one of the integers $$\\label{hei:radical}\n v_q(L_b(u,v))-\\min\\{v_q(u),v_q(v)\\},\\qquad b\\in\\mathcal C,$$ is odd. Here $v_q(q)=1$ and $v_q(0)=+\\infty$. Then $$\\label{hei:height-bound}\n                    h(s)\\le H M_S(s)^c.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-004.1"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal K\\subset\\mathbb Z_{>0}$ be the finite multiplier set supplied by Lemma <a href=\"#pri:pattern\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"pri:pattern\">29</a> in Section <a href=\"#pri:section\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"pri:section\">6</a>. It has the following property. For every integer $K\\ge1$, every $A\\in\\mathbb Z$, and every real lower bound $Y_0>0$, there exist integers $u_1,u_2,v$, with $v\\ne0$, such that, on setting $u_3=Av-u_1-u_2$, the following hold.*\n\n1.  *<span class=\"upright\">Representation and primes.</span> Each of the thirteen numbers $$\\label{pri:coverage-list}\n     v,\\qquad L_b(u_i,v)\n           \\quad(1\\le i\\le3,\\ b\\in\\mathcal C\\setminus\\{\\infty\\})$$ equals $\\varepsilon k r$, where $\\varepsilon\\in\\{1,-1\\}$, $k\\in\\mathcal K$, and $r>Y_0$ is a positive rational prime. Every such $r$ splits in $F$, is a prime of good reduction for both $E$ and $E^\\sigma$, lies outside $S_E$, and satisfies $r>K+1$.*\n\n2.  *<span class=\"upright\">Witnesses at each place.</span> For each occurrence of a prime $r$ in this list and each of the two places $v_r$ of $F$ above it, there are nonzero integers $h,j_0$, chosen independently of $a\\in\\mathbb Z$, such that every $a\\in\\mathbb Z$ satisfies conditions (i) and (ii) of Lemma <a href=\"#red:local-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"red:local-comparison\">4</a> in the ordinary setting: take $R=\\mathbb Z$, its identity embedding in the ordinary structure, $T_a=aP$, $q=r$, and $v_*=v_r$, normalized by $v_r(r)=1$. In this specialization the tildes disappear. The choices of $h,j_0$ may differ at different occurrences and at the two places.*\n\n*The set $\\mathcal K$ is independent of $K,A,Y_0$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-par-pole-parity",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-red-coarsening",
        "exact_quote": "For the prime $q$ and element $r$ in <a href=\"#red:odd-r\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"red:odd-r\">[red:odd-r]</a>, there is a branch $\\mathfrak p\\in\\{\\mathfrak p_+,\\mathfrak p_-\\}$ of that representation axiom and an ambient place $v_*$ of ${}^*F$ over $q$ such that, for every ordinary integer $l\\ge1$ and every $D'\\in F_R$, $$\\label{red:valuation-transfer}\n D'\\in r^l(R_F)_{\\mathfrak p}\n               \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad v_*(\\widetilde D')\\ge l.$$ Every point formal for this tested localization actually reduces to $O$ for $v_*$.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-red-coarsening",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Fixed elliptic data** ([source](#ell:data)): Fix the number field, involution, and elliptic curve $$\label{ell:data} F=\mathbb Q(\sqrt2),\qquad d=75-53\sqrt2,\qquad E:\ y^2=x^3-d^2x,$$ where $\sigma$ is the nontrivial automorphism of $F/\mathbb Q$. The following result, proved in Section <a href="#ell:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ell:section">5</a>, supplies the integer indices.
- **Embedded additive index** ([source](#red:eta)): Define $$\label{red:eta} \widetilde T_a=\eta(a)P.$$
- **Additive index invariants** ([source](#red:additivity)): The group-law axioms give $$\label{red:additivity} \eta(a+a')=\eta(a)+\eta(a'),\qquad \eta(1)=1.$$
- **Odd-contact radical** ([source](#stmt-hei-height)): For $s=(u:v)\in\mathbf P^1(\mathbb Q)\setminus\mathcal C$, let $M_S(s)$ be the product, taken without repetition, of primes $q\notin S$ for which at least one of the integers $$\label{hei:radical} v_q(L_b(u,v))-\min\{v_q(u),v_q(v)\},\qquad b\in\mathcal C,$$ is odd.
- **Recursive ring theory** ([source](#red:theory)): Let $\mathcal T$ be a theory in a countable recursive expansion of the language of rings. Its ring $R$ is required to be an integral domain of characteristic zero with $\sqrt2\notin\mathop{\mathrm{Frac}}(R)$.
- **Tested residue branches** ([source](#red:branch-ideals)): These conditions define two maximal ideals of $R_F$: $$\label{red:branch-ideals} \mathfrak p_\pm =\ker\bigl(R_F\longrightarrow R/rR,\ \sqrt2\longmapsto\pm B_r\bigr).$$
- **Localization membership** ([source](#red:localization-definition)): For $D'\in F_R$, the assertion $$\label{red:localization-definition} D'\in r^i(R_F)_{\mathfrak p_\pm}$$ means that $D'=r^iX/Y$ for some $X,Y\in R_F$ such that the evaluation of $Y$ under $\sqrt2\mapsto\pm B_r$ is nonzero modulo $rR$, hence a unit in that field.
- **Auxiliary twist family** ([source](#pm-h-14)): For $l\in\mathbb Q^\times$, write $$\label{par:curve} E_l:\quad y^2=x(x-l)(x+3l).$$
- **Pole-parity formula** ([source](#pm-h-14)): Define $\Phi(b)$ to hold if $b=0$, or if $b\ne0$ and, for some $m\in\mathcal M$, $m_d\in\mathcal D_m$, and $t,e\in\mathbb Q$, the following conditions hold: $$\label{par:auxiliary-functions} d=m_db,\qquad b'=mb,\qquad U=b\frac{1-dt^2}{1+dt^2},\qquad H'=b'(b'-U^2),$$ $$1+dt^2\ne0,\qquad eH'\ne0,$$ and each of $E_e$ and $E_{eH'}$ has a rational point whose $x$-coordinate belongs to $b'\mathbb Q^{\times2}$.
- **Residue-symbol matrices** ([source](#par:matrices)): At the primes dividing $l$, the local square conditions will be recorded by binary residue symbols. For an odd prime $q$ and a $q$-adic unit $a$, write $[a/q]\in\mathbb F_2$ for the nonsquare bit: it is $0$ for a square residue and $1$ for a nonsquare residue. For a finite set $Q$ of distinct primes different from $2,3$, indexed by $i$, and a vector $w\in\mathbb F_2^Q$, write $D_w$ for the diagonal matrix with diagonal $w$. Put $$\label{par:matrices} s_i=[-1/q_i],\qquad \lambda_i=[3/q_i],\qquad (Ku)_i=\sum_{j\ne i}[q_j/q_i](u_j+u_i),\qquad N=K+D_s.$$ All matrices and vectors in this calculation are over $\mathbb F_2$. Write $\mathbf1$ for the all-ones vector and $\mathbf1_B$ for the indicator of $B$ in $Q$. Quadratic reciprocity gives $$\label{par:reciprocity-matrix} K+K^{\mathsf t}=ss^{\mathsf t}+D_s.$$ In particular, if $s^{\mathsf t}\mathbf1=1$, then $$\label{par:matrix-null-vectors} K\mathbf1=K^{\mathsf t}\mathbf1=0, \qquad N=K^{\mathsf t}+ss^{\mathsf t}.$$
- **Corrected Tate action** ([source](#hei:representation)): On the covariant rational Tate module of $A$ with these extended coefficients put $$\label{hei:representation} R_\ell(g)=\beta(g)\widetilde\mu_{\bar g}\circ g, \qquad g\in G_\mathbb Q,\quad \bar g=g\|_L.$$
- **Uniform sieve denominator** ([source](#stmt-pri-upper-sieve)): . *The number of points in this square avoiding every $\Omega_p$ is at most $$\label{pri:sieve-bound} \frac{n^2}{G(Z)}+O(nZ^6+Z^8),\qquad G(Z)=\sum_{\substack{e\le Z\text{ squarefree}\\p\mid e\Rightarrow p\in\mathcal P_Z}} \prod_{p\mid e}\frac{g(p)}{1-g(p)}.$$
- **Rational solvability set** ([source](#stmt-thm-degree-normal-form)): With the usual effective coding, let $\mathrm{H10}(\mathbb Q)$ be the set of integral polynomials having a rational zero, with the number of variables part of the input.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-fixed-elliptic-rank | Mordell–Weil rank of the fixed elliptic group | = | $1$ | All arithmetic data are fixed. |
| q-curve-parameter | The fixed curve parameter | = | $75-53\sqrt2$ | Independent of the polynomial input. |
| q-parameter-norm | Norm of the fixed curve parameter | = | $7$ | The algebraic integer in the fixed elliptic equation. |
| q-logarithm-truncation-precision | Valuation of the truncated-logarithm index-ratio error | ≥ | $K$ | Uniform in integer point indices and allowed places at fixed precision. |
| q-contact-height-bound | Absolute logarithmic height of a rational base point | ≤ | $H M_S(s)^c$ | The contact forms, exceptions, and positive constants are fixed independently of the point. |
| q-enlarged-exception-height-bound | Height after enlarging the contact-prime exceptions | ≤ | $H\left(\prod_{q\in S'\setminus S}q\right)^c M_{S'}(s)^c$ | The larger exception set is fixed; the exponent is unchanged. |
| q-modeled-polynomial-error | Absolute value of the polynomial evaluated on root indices | ≤ | $C_f B^D$ | The polynomial is fixed; the bounding constant is ordinary. |
| q-required-ordinary-precision | Ordinary precision chosen for the height contradiction | ≥ | $cD$ | The degree and fixed integer height exponent determine the required precision. |
| q-selmer-dimension | Dimension of the auxiliary two-Selmer group | = | $3$ | The support contains the prescribed nonempty support and an additional prime, with the stated kernel and image conditions. |
| q-base-faltings-height-comparison | Height of the image of a family point on the base | ≤ | $C\bigl(1+\max\{0,h_{\mathrm F}(\mathcal A_y)\}\bigr)$ | The constant does not depend on the point or its field of definition. |
| q-splitting-character-order | Order of the finite Dirichlet character splitting the sign obstruction | ≤ | $C M$ | The fixed exceptional set and contact radical determine the ramification set. |
| q-corrected-descent-field-degree | Degree of the field where the corrected action becomes the Tate action | ≤ | $C M$ | The earlier homomorphism field has uniformly bounded degree. |
| q-modular-level | Primitive newform level | ≤ | $C M^a$ | The constants are fixed independently of the rational base point. |
| q-modular-jacobian-dimension | Dimension of the modular Jacobian | ≤ | $C N^2$ | The level varies with the fiber; comparison constants are fixed. |
| q-modular-jacobian-height | Nonnegative part of the modular Jacobian's stable Faltings height | ≤ | $C N^{12}$ | The modular Jacobian has the genus required by the cited height theorem. |
| q-isogeny-degree-estimate | Degree of an isogeny from the already bounded source | ≤ | $\left((14g)^{64g^2}d \max\{h_{\mathrm F}(C_1),\log d,1\}^{2}\right)^{1024g^3}$ | Dimension, field degree, and source height enter the bound; the applications use an already bounded source. |
| q-upper-sieve-error | Number of square lattice points avoiding all forbidden residue sets | ≤ | $\frac{n^2}{G(Z)}+O(nZ^6+Z^8)$ | The error constant is absolute and uniform in the location of the square. |
| q-sieve-cutoff | Sieving cutoff | = | $X^{1/100}$ | The dilation grows after the fixed data and small-prime cutoff have been selected. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-fixed-rank** [claim](#stmt-ell-rank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-auto-remark-3): Two-isogeny descent and local square-class restrictions. -> A fixed cyclic subgroup generated by a nontorsion point.
- **step-formal-ratios** [claim](#stmt-ell-formal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-auto-remark-3-2): Valuation preservation of the formal logarithm and a uniform truncation-tail estimate. -> Parameter-ratio valuations and finite logarithm approximations.
- **step-local-comparison** [claim](#stmt-red-local-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-red-local-comparison): Use the conjugate comparison to prove index-ratio integrality before applying the primary logarithm approximation. -> Integrality and congruence between every embedded value and its index.
- **step-pole-soundness** [claim](#stmt-par-soundness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-par-soundness): Assume an odd pole and compare valuations on the auxiliary twists. -> Even negative valuations for every rational value satisfying the formula.
- **step-cross-squares** [claim](#stmt-par-cross-squares) / [proof](#proof-stmt-par-cross-squares): Weak approximation and strict local cancellation identify mutual square classes. -> Disjoint supports with mutual local-square conditions.
- **step-selmer-descent** [claim](#stmt-par-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-par-descent): Normalize Kummer pairs, solve the residue-matrix constraints, and verify the proposed class locally. -> The required Selmer dimension and additional square-class direction.
- **step-global-point** [claim](#stmt-par-global-point) / [proof](#proof-stmt-par-global-point): Bound full Selmer corank, apply the cited converse, and use the alternating Cassels–Tate pairing. -> A rational point in the required coordinate square class.
- **step-simultaneous-supports** [claim](#stmt-par-simultaneous-matrices) / [proof](#proof-stmt-par-simultaneous-matrices): Prescribe residue symbols, prove deleted-matrix nonsingularity, and realize the symbols by primes. -> A common twist parameter satisfying both descent criteria.
- **step-pole-completeness** [claim](#stmt-par-pole-parity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-par-simultaneous-matrices-2): Apply the rational-point criterion to both twists and transport the second point through the scaling isomorphism. -> An existential formula containing all integers with the required one-sided pole restriction.
- **step-fiber-field** [claim](#stmt-hei-fiber-field) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hei-fiber-field): Use the fixed cover and its local branch equation to analyze specialization ramification. -> A bounded-degree splitting field with controlled ramification and good reduction.
- **step-base-height** [claim](#stmt-hei-base-height) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hei-base-height): Construct a nonconstant theta-null map and compare ample, theta, and Faltings heights. -> A field-independent comparison between base height and fiber height.
- **step-homomorphism-field** [claim](#stmt-hei-hom-field) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hei-hom-field): Take the kernel of the finite Galois action on geometric homomorphism groups. -> A common bounded-degree Galois field defining all homomorphisms.
- **step-sign-splitting** [claim](#stmt-hei-sign-splitting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hei-sign-splitting): Match local Brauer obstructions by Dirichlet characters and remove unwanted inertia with a quadratic correction. -> A bounded-order cochain with a conjugacy-invariant restricted character.
- **step-coefficient-compatibility** [claim](#stmt-hei-compatibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hei-compatibility): Compare algebraic traces from relative Frobenius and reduced quasi-isogenies, then use character identification. -> The same algebraic newform conjugate at both coefficient primes.
- **step-level-bound** [claim](#stmt-hei-level-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hei-level-bound): Separate tame varying primes from a fixed finite set and bound local ramification cuts at the latter. -> A polynomial bound on the primitive modular level.
- **step-modular-factor** [claim](#stmt-hei-modular-factor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hei-modular-factor): Descend representation homomorphisms through scalar extension and apply the homomorphism theorem and complete reducibility. -> The surface as an isogeny factor of the modular Jacobian.
- **step-contact-height** [claim](#stmt-hei-height) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hei-modular-factor-2): Bound CM fibers separately; otherwise bound the modular Jacobian and transfer height through an isogeny with a polarized complement. -> A fixed-power contact-radical bound for rational base height.
- **step-enlarged-exceptions** [claim](#stmt-hei-enlarge-exceptions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hei-enlarge-exceptions): Separate removed primes from the original squarefree contact product. -> The same height exponent with an adjusted constant.
- **step-upper-sieve** [claim](#stmt-pri-upper-sieve) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pri-upper-sieve): Diagonalize the Selberg quadratic form and bound the aggregate residue-count error. -> The upper sieve with an absolute error constant.
- **step-prime-pattern** [claim](#stmt-pri-pattern) / [proof](#pm-h-36): Fix the multiplier catalogue, establish a prime-pattern lower bound, and remove all bad reduction-order common factors by uniform upper sieves. -> Simultaneous prime contact values with compatible reduction orders.
- **step-integer-local-witnesses** [claim](#stmt-pri-integer-tests) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pri-integer-tests): Solve compatible congruences using the two reduction orders and apply formal-ratio estimates. -> Auxiliary integer witnesses fixed for all integer inputs at each place.
- **step-integer-coverage** [claim](#stmt-pri-integer-coverage) / [proof](#proof-stmt-pri-integer-tests-2): Choose a threshold exceeding the fixed exceptions and precision, then attach witnesses to every occurrence and place. -> Ordinary contact representations satisfying all local tests.
- **step-standard-model** [claim](#stmt-red-standard-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-red-standard-model): Interpret point functions by integer multiples and use ordinary contact representations for every axiom instance. -> A recursive theory with an ordinary integer model.
- **step-compactness** [claim](#stmt-red-compactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-red-compactness): Combine successful ground constraints with the ambient elementary diagram and take the term-generated subring. -> An embedded model containing a root and satisfying pole parity throughout.
- **step-coarsening** [claim](#stmt-red-coarsening) / [proof](#proof-stmt-red-coarsening): Quotient the value group by the convex subgroup generated by negative ring values and take the valuation center. -> A tested branch with actual valuation-transfer inequalities.
- **step-index-congruence** [claim](#stmt-red-index-congruence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-red-index-congruence): Transfer the paired branch memberships to valuation hypotheses and invoke the local comparison. -> Index congruences at every contact prime of each represented slope.
- **step-height-contradiction** [claim](#stmt-red-finite-test-success) / [proof](#proof-stmt-red-finite-test-success): Use the same polynomial index error for every represented ring value, bound all ring heights, and compare with elliptic quadratic growth. -> Ordinary integer root coordinates.
- **step-undecidability** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-red-finite-test-success-2): Under hypothetical rational decidability, interleave integer-root enumeration with the search for a failed finite test. -> Contradiction to a total rational-solvability decision algorithm.
- **step-degree-normal-form** [claim](#stmt-thm-degree-normal-form) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-degree-normal-form): Combine the oracle reduction with rational enumeration; translate arithmetic circuits into quadratic gate equations and their squared sum. -> The Turing-degree classification and degree-restricted rational-solvability normal forms.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-circuit-extension**: The gate equations of a supplied finite arithmetic circuit give each rational input a unique extension to its node values. Requiring the output node to vanish preserves rational solvability of the polynomial evaluated by that circuit.
- **invariant-quartic-squared-sum**: The circuit relations, together with the output equation, form a nonempty list of integral polynomials of total degree at most two. Their squared sum has total degree at most four and vanishes over the rationals exactly when all the relations vanish.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-rational-decision-from-normalisation**: Treat the circuit conversion as a terminating decision procedure for rational polynomial solvability. -> The conversion preserves the question of solvability but supplies no general procedure for answering it.
- **anti-fixed-variable-undecidability**: Read the quartic normal form as an undecidability result with a fixed number of variables or bounded coefficient heights. -> The construction introduces node variables and imposes no such bounds.
- **anti-integer-membership-definition**: Extract a single existential definition of the integers in the rationals from the finite-test reduction. -> The source explicitly distinguishes its reduction from such a definition.
- **anti-many-one-completeness**: Promote the reduction from integer solvability to rational solvability to a many-one completeness claim. -> The integer-to-rational reduction remains a Turing reduction; the many-one conversion starts from rational solvability.
- **anti-finite-check-global-proof**: Use successful finite circuit checks to certify the entire undecidability proof or its arithmetic height inputs. -> The executable checks cover the finite circuit construction only; they do not verify the compactness or height arguments.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The executable scope is the finite arithmetic-circuit conversion in the proof of the quartic normal form. The source states: “Given $f\in\mathbb Z[X_1,\ldots,X_n]$, effectively construct an arithmetic circuit evaluating $f$, with input nodes, integral constant nodes, and addition and multiplication gates.” The checks certify exact gate relations and their expanded squared sum on supplied circuits; they do not decide rational solvability or certify the nonconstructive parts of the paper.

```python
# INPUTS: A JSON finite ordered arithmetic circuit with n, nodes, and output.
# PRECONDITIONS: Integral constants; valid input indices; gate references to earlier nodes; a nonempty node list.
# OUTPUTS: Exact integral gate polynomials, an output equation, and their expanded squared sum.
# TIME: Finite symbolic polynomial expansion; an asymptotic bound is ABSENT.
# SPACE: Finite storage of symbolic polynomials; an asymptotic bound is ABSENT.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integer coefficients using symbolic polynomial arithmetic.
# FAILURE: Invalid circuits raise an exception and produce no certificate.
import json
import sys
import sympy as sp


def integer(value):
    if type(value) is not int:
        raise ValueError('integer required')
    return value


def main():
    data = json.load(sys.stdin)
    if type(data) is not dict or set(data) != {'n', 'nodes', 'output'}:
        raise ValueError('invalid circuit fields')
    n = integer(data['n'])
    nodes = data['nodes']
    if n < 0 or type(nodes) is not list or not nodes:
        raise ValueError('invalid circuit size')
    output = integer(data['output'])
    if not 0 <= output < len(nodes):
        raise ValueError('invalid output index')
    xs = [sp.Symbol('X' + str(i)) for i in range(n)]
    ys = [sp.Symbol('Y' + str(i)) for i in range(len(nodes))]
    variables = xs + ys
    q = []
    for i, node in enumerate(nodes):
        if type(node) is not dict:
            raise ValueError('invalid node')
        op = node.get('op')
        y = ys[i]
        if op == 'input' and set(node) == {'op', 'index'}:
            index = integer(node['index'])
            if not 0 <= index < n:
                raise ValueError('invalid input index')
            q.append(y - xs[index])
        elif op == 'const' and set(node) == {'op', 'value'}:
            q.append(y - integer(node['value']))
        elif op in ('add', 'mul') and set(node) == {'op', 'left', 'right'}:
            left = integer(node['left'])
            right = integer(node['right'])
            if not (0 <= left < i and 0 <= right < i):
                raise ValueError('gate is not ordered')
            a, b = ys[left], ys[right]
            if op == 'add':
                q.append(y - a - b)
            else:
                q.append(y - a * b)
        else:
            raise ValueError('invalid node fields or operation')
    q.append(ys[data['output']])
    squared_sum = sp.expand(sum(p * p for p in q))

    def encode(expression):
        polynomial = sp.Poly(expression, *variables, domain=sp.ZZ)
        return [{'powers': list(powers), 'coeff': int(coefficient)}
                for powers, coefficient in polynomial.terms() if coefficient != 0]

    result = {'nvars': len(variables),
              'q': [encode(p) for p in q],
              'sos': encode(squared_sum)}
    print(json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True, separators=(',', ':')))


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()
```

integer(node['right'])\n            require(0 <= left < i and 0 <= right < i)\n            left_poly, right_poly = variable(n + left), variable(n + right)\n            right_hand_side = (add(left_poly, right_poly) if op == 'add'\n                               else multiply(left_poly, right_poly))\n        expected_relations.append(add(variable(n + i), negate(right_hand_side)))\n    expected_relations.append(variable(n + output))\n    expected_squared_sum = {}\n    for relation in expected_relations:\n        expected_squared_sum = add(expected_squared_sum, multiply(relation, relation))\n\n    require(type(candidate) is dict and set(candidate) == {'nvars', 'q', 'sos'})\n    require(integer(candidate['nvars']) == size)\n    require(type(candidate['q']) is list and len(candidate['q']) == len(expected_relations))\n    actual_relations = [decode(p, size) for p in candidate['q']]\n    actual_squared_sum = decode(candidate['sos'], size)\n    require(actual_relations == expected_relations)\n    require(actual_squared_sum == expected_squared_sum)\n    require(all(sum(m) <= 2 for p in actual_relations for m in p))\n    require(all(sum(m) <= 4 for m in actual_squared_sum))\n\n    # Independent direct circuit evaluation on an exhaustive finite rational grid.\n    grid = (Fraction(-1), Fraction(0), Fraction(1), Fraction(1, 2))\n    assignments = itertools.product(grid, repeat=n)\n    for inputs in assignments:\n        values = []\n        for node in nodes:\n            if node['op'] == 'input':\n                value = inputs[node['index']]\n            elif node['op'] == 'const':\n                value = Fraction(node['value'])\n            elif node['op'] == 'add':\n                value = values[node['left']] + values[node['right']]\n            else:\n                value = values[node['left']] * values[node['right']]\n            values.append(value)\n        full_assignment = tuple(inputs) + tuple(values)\n        require(all(evaluate(p, full_assignment) == 0 for p in actual_relations[:-1]))\n        require(evaluate(actual_relations[-1], full_assignment) == values[output])\n        require(evaluate(actual_squared_sum, full_assignment) == values[output] ** 2)\n        for i in range(len(values)):\n            changed = list(full_assignment)\n            changed[n + i] += 1\n            require(evaluate(actual_relations[i], changed) == 1)\n            residuals = [evaluate(p, changed) for p in actual_relations]\n            square_value = evaluate(actual_squared_sum, changed)\n            require(square_value == sum(r * r for r in residuals))\n            require(square_value > 0)\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    try:\n        main()\n    except Exception:\n        sys.exit(1)\n    sys.exit(0)\n",
    "output_contract": "One JSON object on stdout with nvars, q, and sos. Each polynomial is a list of unique nonzero integral terms, each carrying coeff and a nonnegative exponent vector powers of length nvars. The q list contains every node relation in node order followed by the output-node equation. The sos polynomial is the exact expanded sum of their squares. This output encodes an equivalent rational-solvability instance and supplies no solvability verdict.",
    "reason": "The executable scope is the finite arithmetic-circuit conversion in the proof of the quartic normal form. The source states: “Given $f\\in\\mathbb Z[X_1,\\ldots,X_n]$, effectively construct an arithmetic circuit evaluating $f$, with input nodes, integral constant nodes, and addition and multiplication gates.” The checks certify exact gate relations and their expanded squared sum on supplied circuits; they do not decide rational solvability or certify the nonconstructive parts of the paper.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: A JSON finite ordered arithmetic circuit with n, nodes, and output.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Integral constants; valid input indices; gate references to earlier nodes; a nonempty node list.\n# OUTPUTS: Exact integral gate polynomials, an output equation, and their expanded squared sum.\n# TIME: Finite symbolic polynomial expansion; an asymptotic bound is ABSENT.\n# SPACE: Finite storage of symbolic polynomials; an asymptotic bound is ABSENT.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact integer coefficients using symbolic polynomial arithmetic.\n# FAILURE: Invalid circuits raise an exception and produce no certificate.\nimport json\nimport sys\nimport sympy as sp\n\n\ndef integer(value):\n    if type(value) is not int:\n        raise ValueError('integer required')\n    return value\n\n\ndef main():\n    data = json.load(sys.stdin)\n    if type(data) is not dict or set(data) != {'n', 'nodes', 'output'}:\n        raise ValueError('invalid circuit fields')\n    n = integer(data['n'])\n    nodes = data['nodes']\n    if n < 0 or type(nodes) is not list or not nodes:\n        raise ValueError('invalid circuit size')\n    output = integer(data['output'])\n    if not 0 <= output < len(nodes):\n        raise ValueError('invalid output index')\n    xs = [sp.Symbol('X' + str(i)) for i in range(n)]\n    ys = [sp.Symbol('Y' + str(i)) for i in range(len(nodes))]\n    variables = xs + ys\n    q = []\n    for i, node in enumerate(nodes):\n        if type(node) is not dict:\n            raise ValueError('invalid node')\n        op = node.get('op')\n        y = ys[i]\n        if op == 'input' and set(node) == {'op', 'index'}:\n            index = integer(node['index'])\n            if not 0 <= index < n:\n                raise ValueError('invalid input index')\n            q.append(y - xs[index])\n        elif op == 'const' and set(node) == {'op', 'value'}:\n            q.append(y - integer(node['value']))\n        elif op in ('add', 'mul') and set(node) == {'op', 'left', 'right'}:\n            left = integer(node['left'])\n            right = integer(node['right'])\n            if not (0 <= left < i and 0 <= right < i):\n                raise ValueError('gate is not ordered')\n            a, b = ys[left], ys[right]\n            if op == 'add':\n                q.append(y - a - b)\n            else:\n                q.append(y - a * b)\n        else:\n            raise ValueError('invalid node fields or operation')\n    q.append(ys[data['output']])\n    squared_sum = sp.expand(sum(p * p for p in q))\n\n    def encode(expression):\n        polynomial = sp.Poly(expression, *variables, domain=sp.ZZ)\n        return [{'powers': list(powers), 'coeff': int(coefficient)}\n                for powers, coefficient in polynomial.terms() if coefficient != 0]\n\n    result = {'nvars': len(variables),\n              'q': [encode(p) for p in q],\n              'sos': encode(squared_sum)}\n    print(json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True, separators=(',', ':')))\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    main()\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The arithmetic-circuit conversion supplies a concrete preprocessing pass for software handling rational polynomial solvability. Auxiliary node variables turn polynomial evaluation into quadratic gate equations while preserving rational solutions. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The rational-zero decision problem supplies no game dynamics, collision rule, or simulation update construction. The source's elliptic point assignment represents arithmetic indices rather than simulated states. |
| tools | True | strong | implementable | inferred | A symbolic transformation tool can expose the circuit equations as a nonempty quadratic list and expand their squared sum into a quartic polynomial. The list presentation is supplied directly, so the construction requires no recognition procedure for arbitrary sum-of-squares expressions. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The paper gives an effective normalization algorithm with a solution-preserving contract. Its computable transformation is useful independently of the undecidability result, but the source supplies no running-time or storage bound for that transformation. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The circuit construction provides a concrete representation connecting polynomial inputs, node variables, and local gate equations. This representation retains the information needed to extend a rational input uniquely to all intermediate values. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The effective circuit normalization describes algebraic dependencies, but supplies no hardware mapping, parallel execution schedule, or accelerator resource contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source establishes undecidability for unrestricted rational solvability, rather than a cryptographic construction with keys, distributions, or a security reduction. Its oracle reduction does not establish the stronger many-one completeness claim either. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The squared-sum construction expresses exact simultaneous rational equations. The source gives no training procedure or guarantee that approximate minimization produces an exact rational zero. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | For exact rational polynomial feasibility, the construction converts an arbitrary polynomial equality into quadratic equalities and then into a nonnegative quartic expression whose zero set represents the same feasibility question. Adapting it to an optimisation system requires retaining exact rational semantics; the paper supplies no optimisation method or approximate convergence guarantee. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The interleaved searches in the undecidability proof assume a rational-solvability oracle and establish a logical reduction. They supply no distributed communication, consensus, or fault-tolerance construction. |
| other (Computability and exact arithmetic verification) | True | strong | implementable | stated | Enumeration of rational tuples followed by exact polynomial evaluation gives a concrete positive-instance semidecision procedure. Finding a rational zero supplies a checkable witness, while continued enumeration gives no certificate of nonexistence. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-rational-field-unrestricted-input** (formal_scope): The undecidability conclusion concerns rational zeros of integral polynomials with the number of variables supplied as part of the input. It does not establish undecidability for every fixed-variable subclass or for polynomial solvability over other fields. Blocked use: ['Transfer the theorem to real or complex solvability without a separate argument.', 'Assert undecidability for a fixed number of variables solely from the main theorem.']
- **limit-degree-restriction-retains-unbounded-parameters** (formal_scope): The quartic and quadratic-list restrictions retain unrestricted numbers of variables, summands, and coefficient heights. The quadratic list is supplied as input rather than recovered by recognizing an arbitrary polynomial presentation. Blocked use: ['Advertise the normal form as a bounded-variable undecidability theorem.', 'Assume a recognition procedure for arbitrary sum-of-squares presentations.']
- **limit-oracle-reduction-not-many-one-completeness** (formal_scope): The reduction from integer solvability remains a Turing reduction. The many-one reductions to restricted inputs begin with rational solvability, so the proof does not establish many-one completeness of rational solvability. Blocked use: ['Replace the integer-to-rational oracle reduction by a many-one reduction.', 'Claim many-one completeness from the Turing-degree classification.']
- **limit-finite-test-search-requires-rational-oracle** (hypothesis): The interleaved integer-root and failed-test searches give a terminating integer decision procedure only under the assumed rational-solvability decision procedure or corresponding oracle. The reduction does not supply that oracle. Blocked use: ['Implement the conditional failed-test search as an unconditional total rational solver.', 'Interpret a finite collection of successful tests as success of every test.']
- **limit-positive-enumeration-no-negative-certificate** (edge_case): Exact rational-tuple enumeration eventually finds an existing rational zero, but failure to find one in a finite search supplies no certificate of nonexistence. Blocked use: ['Report unsolvability after a bounded enumeration finds no witness.', 'Promise termination of rational-tuple enumeration on every polynomial.']
- **limit-nonconstant-test-construction** (edge_case): The finite-test integer-root implication is stated for nonconstant polynomials. Constant inputs are handled directly before that construction is applied. Blocked use: ['Apply the nonconstant finite-test argument to constant polynomials without its separate constant-case handling.']
- **limit-fixed-data-existence-not-uniform-extraction** (nonconstructive): The recursive theory uses fixed arithmetic constants whose existence permits hardcoding. The proof does not require an effective search for those choices, and the CM height argument invokes finiteness that need not be effective. Blocked use: ['Claim a supplied uniform procedure computing every fixed arithmetic choice or numerical height constant.', 'Treat existence of fixed constants as an executed computation of them.']
- **limit-compactness-model-is-existence-only** (nonconstructive): The elementary diagram is used to obtain an existence model, is not required to be recursive, and is never queried by the finite tests. Compactness is not a finite model-extraction algorithm here. Blocked use: ['Construct an executable solver by enumerating the complete elementary diagram.', 'Use finite circuit checks as a certificate for existence of the compactness model.']
- **limit-index-map-not-multiplicative** (hypothesis): The elliptic index map is injective and additive, but multiplicativity is not assumed. Polynomial evaluation on ring values cannot be replaced directly by polynomial evaluation on their indices before the local and height arguments. Blocked use: ['Treat the index assignment as a ring homomorphism.', 'Infer an integer root solely from the modeled root and additivity.']
- **limit-paired-local-comparison-uniform-witnesses** (hypothesis): The local comparison requires both tests at the same ambient place, with nonzero auxiliary elements chosen independently of the ring element. The allowed prime exclusions, ordinary precision, reduction conditions, and nonzero denominators are essential. Blocked use: ['Drop the conjugate comparison before establishing integrality of the primary index ratio.', 'Choose different primary auxiliary elements for the ring element and the multiplicative identity.', 'Combine the paired comparisons at unrelated places.']
- **limit-transfer-finite-polynomials-only** (formal_scope): Transfer applies to assertions about integer multiples of the fixed points and finite logarithm polynomials. No infinite formal series is evaluated in the elementary extension. Blocked use: ['Evaluate the infinite formal logarithm directly on nonordinary points.', 'Remove the integrality and prime-size conditions from the truncated-logarithm estimate.']
- **limit-pole-parity-is-one-sided** (hypothesis): The existential formula excludes odd negative valuations outside its fixed exceptional set. It does not exclude even poles and does not by itself define integer membership. Blocked use: ['Conclude that every rational value satisfying the formula is an integer.', 'Apply its pole restriction at excluded primes without additional evidence.']
- **limit-no-single-integer-membership-definition** (formal_scope): The finite-test reduction and circuit conversion do not supply a single existential definition of the integers in the rationals. Blocked use: ['Publish the reduction as an explicit Diophantine integer-membership predicate.', 'Replace the sequence of finite tests by an asserted equivalent single existential membership condition.']
- **limit-external-valuation-coarsening** (nonconstructive): The convex subgroup used for valuation coarsening is externally defined and serves to construct a valuation. It is not an algorithmic object or a transferred formula. Blocked use: ['Treat the convex subgroup as an effectively enumerable component of the finite tests.', 'Assume a tested localization already embeds in the original ambient valuation ring before coarsening.']
- **limit-height-contact-regime-and-fixed-exceptions** (hypothesis): The contact-height estimate concerns rational projective points outside the fixed contact set, with normalized contact orders and fixed exceptional primes. Enlarging the exceptional set changes the constant and preserves the exponent only within the stated fixed finite regime. Blocked use: ['Apply the radical definition at an excluded contact point.', 'Use an input-dependent enlargement while retaining the original uniform constant.', 'Replace normalized odd contact by arbitrary prime support.']
- **limit-selmer-direction-needs-global-realization** (hypothesis): The matrix criterion provides a Selmer class, not yet a rational point. The global-point argument first verifies the full Selmer-corank bound, invokes the cited finiteness theorem, and then uses the perfect alternating pairing on the full finite primary group. Blocked use: ['Identify every Selmer class with a rational point without controlling the Tate–Shafarevich obstruction.', 'Apply perfection of the pairing directly to its subgroup killed by two.', 'Use the rational-point conclusion without the twist-support and matrix hypotheses.']
- **limit-modularity-and-conductor-preconditions** (hypothesis): The modularity step requires verification of irreducibility, oddness, finite ramification, and the distinct de Rham weights. The conductor argument uses a different coefficient prime at the dyadic place, with compatibility established for the same algebraic newform conjugate. Blocked use: ['Apply the modularity input to an arbitrary continuous representation.', 'Compare the dyadic conductor using the dyadic representation without the separate compatibility argument.', 'Substitute an unrelated newform conjugate in the conductor comparison.']
- **limit-isogeny-height-source-already-bounded** (hypothesis): The quantitative isogeny estimate is applied to a source whose height has already been bounded. The height transfer also uses a lower bound for the complement, so the final surface bound is not obtained merely by exhibiting an isogeny factor. Blocked use: ['Apply the isogeny estimate with the unknown target height and claim an independent bound.', 'Discard the complement height without its lower-bound argument.']
- **limit-prime-pattern-order-of-choices** (hypothesis): The multiplier set is fixed before the integer input and cutoff; dilation is taken large only after those choices. The asymptotic argument does not require uniformity of its error as the input or cutoff varies. Blocked use: ['Claim a uniform numerical search bound over all integer inputs and cutoffs.', 'Let the cutoff vary with dilation while using the fixed-parameter asymptotic theorem unchanged.']
- **limit-upper-sieve-hypotheses-and-error** (hypothesis): The upper sieve assumes the specified forbidden residue subsets, density regime, squarefree support, and lattice square. Its bound retains the residue-count error; the main term alone is not the stated upper estimate. Blocked use: ['Discard the error term without showing it is controlled in the intended regime.', 'Apply the estimate to arbitrary forbidden densities or arbitrary sampling regions.']
- **limit-circuit-normalization-exact-rational-semantics** (approximation): The circuit conversion preserves exact rational solvability through its gate equations and output equation. Replacing the equations by their squared sum relies on nonnegativity of rational squares; small numerical residuals are not rational-zero certificates. Blocked use: ['Drop the output equation or a gate relation.', 'Replace exact vanishing by approximate residual minimization.', 'Transfer the squared-sum equivalence to a field where squares can cancel.']
- **limit-finite-executable-coverage** (formal_scope): Finite checks of arithmetic-circuit normalization establish only the checked polynomial identities and circuit instances. They do not certify the compactness construction, the height estimate, the external arithmetic inputs, or the global undecidability theorem. Blocked use: ['Promote a finite normalization receipt to verification of the entire paper.', 'Treat agreement on tested circuits as a proof covering every circuit.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We give a negative answer to Hilbert's tenth problem over the rational numbers: no algorithm decides whether a polynomial with integer coefficients has a rational zero. The number of variables is part of the input.
